Nothing gets built until every source has been checked and every gap has\nbeen named.\n\n**Intended model: Opus 5 at maximum effort.** A skill cannot pin the session model — set it\nyourself before invoking. The plan synthesis step delegates to the `zm-planner` agent, which is\npinned to Opus regardless of the session model, so the expensive judgement runs on Opus either\nway.\n\n**Input:** `SFCC-83 and SFCC-84 (Recently Viewed UI Component + Recently Viewed See Full History), being folded into the existing feature/SFCC-236_product-card-shared-component branch. SFCC-84's card behaviour is confirmed to reuse the existing 'plp' variation; SFCC-83's rail is confirmed to exclude both the badge and the hover model-photo swap per Dani Rivett's scope note.` — a Jira key (e.g. `SFCC-194`), optionally followed by a depth.\nIf no key is given, ask for one before doing anything else.\n\n## Depth\n\nDepth is a parameter, not an adverb. Pick one and say which you are using:\n\n| Depth | Jira + Confluence | Codebase | Figma |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `quick` | the ticket and its direct links | branch state, file tiers | the named node only |\n| `standard` *(default)* | ticket, links, comments; targeted Confluence search | tiers, blast radius, nearest existing pattern | node, variants, states |\n| `exhaustive` | full link graph, all comments, related epics, all matching Confluence | above, plus every consumer of every touched file and the exemplar PR diffs | node, all variants, all states, all viewports, token mapping |\n\nNever substitute a stronger adverb for a depth. \"In-depth full-on exhaustive\" is not a level;\n`exhaustive` is.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 0 — position\n\nCall `/session-start`. It confirms the branch, reads the tracking docs, runs health checks and\nstates current position.\n\nThen confirm one thing it does not: **you cannot be on `develop` or `main`.** A `PreToolUse` hook\nblocks every Write and Edit on those branches, so a plan handed to the builder from `develop` is\ndead on arrival. If the branch is wrong, say so and stop — do not create a branch unasked.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 1 — gather\n\nRun these **in parallel**. Each one ends in its own findings block. Never let a clean source hide\na broken one — report all five, even when four are fine.\n\n### Jira — `mcp__mcp-atlassian__jira_get_issue`\n\nPull the story and read all of it: description, requirements, acceptance criteria, linked design,\nattachments, linked work items, and the full comment thread. Comments routinely carry the\ndecision that contradicts the description.\n\nFlag: missing or untestable ACs · requirements that contradict each other or the comments ·\nno linked design · attachments referenced in prose but not attached · linked work items that are\nstill open and block this one · a decision made in comments that never made it into the description.\n\n### Confluence — `confluence_search`, `confluence_get_page`\n\nSearch for anything bearing on this story: TSDs, integration docs, data dictionaries, decisions.\n\nFlag: no TSD where the work clearly needs one · a TSD that contradicts the ticket · documentation\ndescribing an approach the codebase does not implement · a page last updated before a decision\nthat supersedes it.\n\n### FED handbook — `~/Dev/fed-handbook.md`\n\nThe build approach: tokens, components, CSS structure, overriding, accessibility, tests, stories.\nPart I is the machine, Part II the procedure, Part III the role.\n\nFlag: handbook guidance that contradicts what the codebase now does · a claim whose cited\n`file:line` no longer resolves · anything the story needs that the handbook does not cover.\n\nThe handbook is a derived account, not the source. **Where it disagrees with\n`.claude/rules/overrideStyling.md`, that file wins** — and say so, because it means the handbook\nis stale.\n\n### Codebase\n\nBranch state, and the shape of what is about to be touched.\n\n- Confirm `develop` is current with `origin/develop`.\n- For every likely-touched file: tier and owner, from `.claude/rules/frozenFiles.md` and\n  `protectedFiles.md`. **Never from `.claude/registry/*.md`** — its Tier column is a derived\n  heuristic and is wrong in places.\n- Call `/audit-tier <path>` for anything you are unsure about. It also returns import blast radius.\n- Find the nearest existing pattern. This codebase has strong precedent; matching it is cheaper\n  and more reviewable than inventing.\n- Read the exemplar PRs when the story touches their surface: **PR #64** (ui-kit atom/molecule,\n  four-file pattern, co-located recipe, render-slot wiring), **PR #59** (global-chrome component,\n  co-located recipe with shared cross-file values), **PR #56** (pure token layer, no component\n  change).\n\nFlag: any Tier 1 file in the likely set · high blast radius (a shared file with many consumers) ·\nwork that crosses squad territory · an existing pattern the story would contradict.\n\n### Figma\n\n- Design System 2026 — `https://www.figma.com/design/wPiWmZZOVKnrENUWk2XWWm/Zimmermann_Design_System_2026`\n- Zimmermann_Designs — `https://www.figma.com/design/QQ427wHWEUjsjP7H8v48Rs/Zimmermann_Designs`\n\n**Ask for one node URL per variant and per state**, not one per component. If the file-level\nlinks are not enough to resolve the specific component, stop and ask Marcel. Do not guess at\nwhich node the story means, and do not build from a neighbouring node that looks close.\n\nCheck:\n\n- **Completeness** — every variant and every state: hover, focus, active, disabled, loading,\n  error, empty, success.\n- **Currency** — is this design archived or superseded? Triage archived against current before\n  anyone builds to it. An archived node that still renders is a trap.\n- **Tokenisation** — does every value map to an existing `--zimm-*` token, or is a new token\n  being introduced? A new token is Tier 1 and needs the designer.\n- **Responsiveness** — frames exist at 402 / 1023 / 1440; breakpoint tokens are 768 / 860 / 1024.\n  **The 768–1022 band is unframed**, so anything in it is an inference, not a design.\n\nTwo measurement traps that have produced false defects here. Flag a comparison that hits either:\n\n- **Node type decides comparability.** Figma TEXT nodes report a cap-height box, which is not\n  comparable to a CSS line box — never file a mismatch from one. FRAMES and INSTANCES report a\n  real box and are directly comparable.\n- **The two Figma files must never be cross-compared.** The Design System file and the Designs\n  file are different sources; a delta between them is not a defect.\n\n**No design is a blocker, not licence to invent.** If the ACs name something Figma does not have,\nthat is a finding for Marcel — or an explicit **provisional**, labelled as such and carried into\nthe plan as an assumption. Never fill the gap silently.\n\nFlag: missing variants or states · a design that is archived or superseded · values with no token\n· the unframed tablet band left to inference · a design that contradicts an established component\n· no design at all where the story implies one.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 2 — reuse\n\nThese already exist. Call them; do not reimplement them.\n\n1. `/feature-spec` — turns the gathered picture into a scoped implementation plan and emits a\n   ready-to-paste scoped prompt.\n2. `/scope-check` — run it **on that prompt**. It verdicts SAFE / RISKY / BLOCKED and rewrites\n   risky prompts. This is the highest-leverage gate available; do not skip it.\n3. `/audit-tier <path>` — per file, wherever scope-check flags something specific.\n\nTwo known traps when calling these:\n\n- `/code-quality` defaults to diffing against **`main`**; `/pr-checklist` diffs against\n  **`develop`**. On a feature branch these give different file sets. Always pass explicit scope.\n- `/pr-checklist` already calls `/code-quality` internally. Calling both doubles the audit.\n\nNone of these skills emits machine-readable output — they all return prose for a human. If you\nneed to branch on a result, restate the verdict yourself rather than parsing theirs.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 3 — the gate\n\nThis is the part that earns the skill. Produce one table, every source, every gap:\n\n| # | Source | Finding | Severity | What unblocks it |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n\nSeverity is one of:\n\n- 🔴 **BLOCKED** — cannot plan around it. A missing AC, an absent design, a Tier 1 file with no\n  owner approval, a `scope-check` BLOCKED verdict. **Stop here.** Report and wait for Marcel.\n- 🟡 **CLARIFY** — plannable, but the plan carries a stated assumption. Name the assumption\n  explicitly in the plan so it is visible if it turns out wrong.\n- 🔵 **NOTE** — worth knowing, does not affect the plan.\n\n**Do not fill a gap with an assumption and carry on silently.** A missing acceptance criterion is\na finding, not something to infer. The whole point of the gate is that it stops.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 4 — plan\n\nDelegate to the `zm-planner` agent (Opus). ''` renders an empty element that **keeps its margins**, measured as a roughly 64px void.\"\n&gt; \"- **Fix at the atom**, not at each call site.\"\n\n**Part III §\"Shipping discipline\" → \"Three states are AC, not polish\"** — the most directly actionable thing in the handbook for SFCC-83:\n\n&gt; \"Every component that renders fetched data needs **loading** (a skeleton, not a spinner), **error** (with retry), and **absent** (see Part I §7) specified in the story. Data-fetching hooks, container/presentational, mapper placement\n\n**Part II §1 — \"Static-first\"** (the canonical statement):\n\n&gt; \"Props in, markup out. A component under `overrides/app/components/` receives its data and renders it. It does not fetch.\"\n\n&gt; \"Mock data goes in the story — never a temporary fetch you intend to remove. A component that owns its own data-fetching cannot be reused where the data arrives differently: SSR-prefetched, mocked in Storybook, or composed by a parent that already has it.\"\n\n&gt; \"**The one exception**, and it is narrow: Context-backed, app-wide static-content hooks (`useIntl`, `useUiCopy`) may be called from any component, including a presentational leaf.\"\n\n&gt; \"⚠️ **Their no-Provider behaviour differs and it will bite you in tests.** `useUiCopy` degrades to fallback text and renders with zero setup. **`useIntl` throws**, and its `IntlProvider` is mounted by the base SFCC `_app`, not `_app-config` — a narrower subtree than you'd expect. Any Vitest test rendering a `useIntl` consumer must wrap it.\"\n\n**Part II §1 — \"Leverage what already exists\"** — the rung ladder, and the reason to check `product-scroller`/`recommended-products` before building:\n\n&gt; \"1. **Use the base SFCC component as-is.** 2. **Override it** … 3. **Compose existing ui-kit atoms** into something new. 4. **Build a new component.** Rarest. Nothing else on the PDP ties an external field name to a rendered prop, and a mis-keyed field here does not fail loudly: it falls through to `undefined`, the component returns `null`, and the page renders a plausible-looking product with one line quietly missing. Architecture decisions\n91\t\n92\t**Variation axis = discriminated union (data contract) + declarative config table (layout).**\n93\t`typescript.md` mandates the union, and the ACs require it: Wishlist alone needs `colour` + `size` + mandatory\n94\t`onRemove`; PLP alone needs `sizes` + `onNotifyMe`. A flat optional-everything bag lets you build a Wishlist\n95\tcard with no remove CTA (AC violation) and a PLP card with one (design violation).\n96\t\n97\t```\n98\tZimmProductCardProps = Base & (PlpVariation | HomeVariation | WishlistVariation)\n99\t  base:       id, href, name, headingLevel, primaryImage, hoverImage?, images?,\n100\t              currentPrice?, originalPrice?, isUnavailable?, badge?\n101\t  'plp':      sizes?, selectedSize?, onSizeSelect?, onAddToBag?, onNotifyMe?\n102\t  'home':     —\n103\t  'wishlist': colour, size, onRemove (required), onAddToBag?\n104\t```\n105\t\n106\t`'recently-viewed'` is **not** a fourth member — the AC says it behaves identically to PLP, so consumers pass\n107\t`variation: 'plp'`. Recorded as an assumption, not a silent choice.\n108\t\n109\tThe render body must **not** `switch` on `props.variation`. Layout comes from a module-level `VARIATION_CONFIG`\n110\t(`showBag`, `showAttributes`, `showRemove`, `hoverHidesPrice`, `hoverRevealsPrice`, `hoverRevealsSizeSelector`,\n111\t`hoverSwapsImage`). That is what satisfies the extensibility AC: add a union member + a config row, touch no JSX.\n112\t\n113\t**The card is fluid, not 288px.** `gridSystem.ts` has `maxWidth: 'none'`. Figma's 288/179 are widths at the\n114\treference artboards. The card fills its grid cell (`width: 100%`); the image holds ratio via `aspectRatio`.\n115\tGrid placement belongs to SFCC-80.\n116\t\n117\t**Mobile/desktop boundary = 768px** (`breakpoints.md`, 48em) — the only confirmed foundation breakpoint between\n118\tthe 402 and 1440 artboards, and where the grid already goes 4→8 columns. `min-width` is inclusive, so the 768\n119\tartboard gets the desktop card. Every hover rule is additionally gated on `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`\n120\tso a 768px touch tablet never gets hover-only content. See OQ-5.\n121\t\n122\t**Hover is CSS-only, keyed on `:hover, :focus-within`.** No React hover state — 60 tiles re-rendering on pointer\n123\tmove is a PLP perf defect. `:focus-within` is not optional: WCAG 2.2 AA requires the revealed size selector and\n124\tarrows be keyboard-reachable. All reveal selectors live in one place, the organism's recipe root part, selecting\n125\tdescendants by the shared `data-card-slot` constants. Molecules stay hover-agnostic.\n126\t\n127\t**One link per card (stretched-link).** The card holds a bag button, arrows, size options and a remove button —\n128\tnesting those in an `<a>` is invalid HTML and an a11y failure. The link wraps the product name only and carries\n129\ta `::after { position:absolute; inset:0 }` overlay; controls get `position:relative; z-index:1`. `href` arrives\n130\tpre-prefixed from the page. Uses `import {Link as RouteLink} from 'react-router-dom'` per `zimmColourSwatch` —\n131\tthere is no `overrides/app/components/link/` override, contrary to a claim in `overrideStyling.md`.\n132\t\n133\t**Reuse, don't rebuild:** `ZimmProductName`, `ZimmProductPrice`, `ZimmIcon` (`bag`, `close`, `chevron-left`,\n134\t`chevron-right` all exist in `ICON_MAP`). No new icons.\n135\t\n136\t**VIC pricing is not a card concern** — precedence is the page mapper's job. Deferred to SFCC-778 (OQ-6).\n137\t\n138\t---\n139\t\n140\t## 4a. Arrows vs. indicator — RESOLVED (Marcel, 2026-09-02)\n141\t\n142\t> *\"position indicator is just for tablet and mobile, desktop hover is arrows\"*\n143\t\n144\tThe two are **mutually exclusive**, not paired. Consequences for Steps 1 and 4:\n145\t\n146\t**Split by pointer capability, not viewport width.** The gate is\n147\t`@media (hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` — the same gate every other hover rule already uses:\n148\t\n149\t- **hover-capable + fine pointer** → prev/next arrow buttons, revealed on `:hover, :focus-within`. No indicator.\n150\t- **everything else (touch tablet, mobile)** → `ZimmSlideIndicator`, always visible. No arrows.\n151\t\n152\tCapability rather than width is what actually delivers the stated intent: the 768 artboard is a *touch* tablet,\n153\tand a width rule alone would give it arrows it can never hover to reveal. It also keeps this consistent with the\n154\tcard's own hover rules rather than introducing a second, differently-keyed breakpoint. **Edge case, accepted:**\n155\ta desktop browser resized below 768 keeps arrows (it still has a fine pointer); a touch-screen laptop gets the\n156\tindicator. Both are the safe failure direction — nobody is shown a control they cannot operate.\n157\t\n158\t**Two knock-on changes to the step pass criteria:**\n159\t\n160\t1. **Step 1 — the indicator is NOT `aria-hidden` on touch.** The original criterion assumed the arrow buttons\n161\t   announce position, so the indicator could be hidden as duplicate. Where the indicator renders there are **no\n162\t   arrows**, so it becomes the only position signal — hiding it would be a 1.1.1 information loss. It needs an\n163\t   accessible position announcement (e.g. a visually-hidden \"Image 3 of 8\" live region, or `role=\"group\"` with\n164\t   `aria-label`). Revised criterion: exposed and announced when it is the sole affordance.\n165\t2. **Step 4 — mobile image paging has no mechanism and no AC.** If the indicator shows position on touch, some\n166\t   touch gesture must change the image. No AC covers this; the ACs only describe desktop hover. See OQ-11.\n167\t\n168\t---\n169\t\n170\t## 4b. Touch image paging — RESOLVED (Marcel, 2026-09-02)\n171\t\n172\t> *\"scroll-snap it is\"*\n173\t\n174\tNative CSS scroll-snap. The dots remain a **passive readout**, visually unchanged from Figma — no enlargement,\n175\tno conversion to tap targets, so no departure from the design and no WCAG 2.5.8 problem to solve.\n176\t\n177\t**Implementation shape: one scroll container on every breakpoint, not two mechanisms.**\n178\t\n179\tThe image strip is always a horizontally scrollable flex container with `scrollSnapType: 'x mandatory'` and\n180\t`scrollSnapAlign: 'start'` on each slide. What differs by pointer capability is only the *affordance* layered\n181\tover it:\n182\t\n183\t| | Mechanism | Affordance |\n184\t|---|---|---|\n185\t| hover + fine pointer | same scroll container | arrows call `scrollBy()`; no indicator |\n186\t| touch | same scroll container | native drag; indicator reflects position |\n187\t\n188\tOne container rather than two code paths means a single source of truth for \"which image is showing\", the\n189\tarrows become progressive enhancement over something that already works, and the desktop path inherits\n190\tkeyboard scrollability for free.\n191\t\n192\t**Active-index derivation:** prefer `IntersectionObserver` on the slides over a scroll listener — 60 tiles on a\n193\tPLP make a per-scroll-event listener a real perf cost, and the repo already has\n194\t`overrides/app/hooks/use-intersection-observer.ts` (**Tier 2 — import it, do not modify it**). The `scrollend`\n195\tevent is an acceptable alternative. Left to the Implementer; either satisfies the pass criteria.\n196\t\n197\t**Precedent:** the base `ProductScroller`\n198\t(`node_modules/@salesforce/retail-react-app/app/components/product-scroller/index.jsx:79-84`) already uses\n199\t`scrollSnapType: 'x mandatory'` with `overflowX: 'scroll'`. Same technique, already proven in this codebase.\n200\t\n201\t**Accepted caveat:** swipe has no explicit affordance beyond the dots implying further content. This is the\n202\tuniversal mobile convention, and the alternative (enlarging 1px dots into 24×24 tap targets) trades an\n203\tinvisible-but-expected gesture for a visible-but-off-design control. **Action: flag to Kiera that the mobile\n204\tgesture is undocumented in the design**, so it lands in the spec rather than being discovered in QA.\n205\t\n206\t**Added Step 4 pass criteria:**\n207\t- The slide container carries `scrollSnapType: 'x mandatory'`; every slide carries `scrollSnapAlign`.\n208\t- Arrows are rendered only under `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`; the indicator only outside it; assert the\n209\t  two never coexist in the same DOM.\n210\t- Arrow activation moves the scroll position (assert `scrollBy`/`scrollTo` is called — jsdom does not lay out,\n211\t  so assert the call, not the resulting offset).\n212\t- The indicator's active index tracks the observed slide, and clamps at both ends.\n213\t- A single-image product renders no indicator, no arrows, and no horizontal overflow.\n214\t- No gesture library is added — `package.json` is untouched (AGENTS.md Hard Rule #7).\n215\t\n216\t---\n217\t\n218\t## 4c. Badge — RESOLVED (Marcel, 2026-09-02), and it corrects the decomposition\n219\t\n220\tNine nodes supplied; all resolve off one parent, `40001471:20702` \"Badge\" (a documentation board).\n221\t`40001471:20682` is its cover slide, not a component.\n222\t\n223\t**There are TWO badge components, not one:**\n224\t\n225\t| Component set | Node | Variants | Size |\n226\t|---|---|---|---|\n227\t| **`Badge`** | `40001471:20752` | `State=Primary` (`…20751`) · `State=Hover` (`…20750`) | 33×15 |\n228\t| **`Stock Status Badge`** | `40001796:7041` | `State=Available` (`…7040`) · `State=Unavailable` (`…7039`) · `State=Low stock` (`…7038`) | 66–79×20 |\n229\t\n230\t`Stock Status Badge` appears in **no acceptance criterion**. The union still carries `onRemove` on the wishlist member; the organism passes it to Media.\n255\t3. **This makes the split cleaner, not messier:** everything overlaid on the image is Media, everything below\n256\t   it is Content.\n257\t4. **§4a and §4b are confirmed by the design itself,** not just by the verbal decision — `Size=Large` has\n258\t   `Carousel` and no indicator; `Size=Small` has `Slide-indicator` and no `Carousel`.\n259\t5. **The slide indicator is a 1px hairline flush to the image's bottom edge**, not floating dots. Step 1's\n260\t   recipe must not assume dot geometry.\n261\t\n262\t### Two new findings that need raising, not resolving in code\n263\t\n264\t- **🟡 The badge looks interactive, against a requirement that says it must not be.** `Tag` wraps its label in\n265\t  a **`touchContainer`** — this file's convention for an interactive hit area (the bag icon and both arrows use\n266\t  the identical wrapper) — and the `Badge` component ships a **`State=Hover`**. Branch not yet cut (skill rule: never create a branch unasked).\n**Scope: SFCC-236 only.** SFCC-83 and SFCC-84 are held — blocked on data source, surface list, and Legal's consent ruling.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Goal\n\nBuild the shared Zimmermann Product Card per SFCC-236 (8 pts, Sprint 4, epic SFCC-2), designed in Figma\n`wPiWmZZOVKnrENUWk2XWWm` set `40000223:2822` (20 variants: Size × State × Type × Unavailable), following the\nFED handbook's ui-kits atomic layering and the PR #64 / `zimmColourSwatchGroup` pattern — named-export\ncomponent reading a `Zimm*`-namespaced multipart recipe via `useMultiStyleConfig`, parts applied with\n`<chakra.div __css={styles.x}>`, all values `var(--zimm-*)` with explicit media queries.\n\n**The card is the visual layer only:** every value arrives as a prop, it owns no data fetching, and it renders\nthe add-to-bag / size-selection controls without wiring their behaviour (SFCC-185 / SFCC-497 / SFCC-90).\n\n---\n\n## 2. Scale call — one branch, seven commits, one PR\n\n```bash\ngit fetch origin && git checkout -b feature/SFCC-236_product-card-shared-component origin/develop\n```\n\nThe current checkout (`feature/SFCC-91_...`) is merged and must not be built on.\n\n**Why one branch despite ~24 files:** one component family, one Figma set, one ticket, one reviewer set\n(`pwa-squad-a` + `pwa-squad-b` on every file). The molecules have exactly one consumer — the organism — so\nmerging them separately merges dead code. Splitting would also multiply the single Tier 2 touch\n(`theme/components/index.ts`) across three approval cycles for an append-only registry edit.\n\n**Full build loop (Implementer → Tester → Scorer per step).** This is a keystone blocking eight tickets, so a\nwrong prop contract propagates into eight downstream stories.\n\n### Deliberately dropped\n\n| Dropped | Why |\n|---|---|\n| Copy-first | Nothing to copy — no file is being overridden at a mirrored path |\n| Playwright / `.feature` | No page renders this component on this branch; E2E belongs to SFCC-80/90/497 |\n| Bundle-size / LCP step | Nothing imports the card yet; bundle delta is zero |\n| Visual regression | Chromatic/Loki is out of budget (`testing.md` excludes it); hover fidelity is a manual Storybook check |\n| Multi-region fixtures | The card takes pre-formatted strings; no locale/currency logic to vary |\n\n**Retained, non-negotiable:** `npx tsc --noEmit` · `npm run test` · `npm run lint` · `npm run audit:tokens` ·\n`cr review --light --base develop`.\n\n---\n\n## 3. File manifest\n\n### Modified (3)\n\n| File | Tier | Owner |\n|---|---|---|\n| `overrides/app/theme/components/index.ts` | **Tier 2** | `tier-2-approvers` + `tier-1-approvers` (CODEOWNERS:117) |\n| `overrides/app/components/ui-kits/atoms/zimmProductPrice/index.tsx` | 3/4 | `pwa-squad-a` + `pwa-squad-b` |\n| `overrides/app/components/ui-kits/atoms/zimmProductPrice/index.test.tsx` | 3/4 | same |\n\n**The Tier 2 file is the only tier crossing.** AGENTS.md Hard Rule #10 → needs explicit scope confirmation\nbefore Step 1. No Tier 1 file is touched.\n\n### Created (21) — all Tier 3/4, `pwa-squad-a` + `pwa-squad-b`\n\nRoot `overrides/app/components/ui-kits/`, each with `index.tsx` · `index.stories.tsx` · `index.test.tsx` · `<name>.css.ts`:\n\n```\nmolecules/zimmSlideIndicator/\natoms/zimmSizeChip/\nmolecules/zimmSizeChipGroup/\nmolecules/zimmProductCardMedia/\nmolecules/zimmProductCardContent/\norganisms/zimmProductCard/          + zimmProductCardShared.ts\n```\n\n**Explicitly NOT created: `overrides/app/components/product-tile/`** — creating it activates the resolver for\nPLP, Search, Home, Cart, PDP and empty-search-results, and `product-list/index.jsx:51-53` imports\n`{Skeleton as ProductTileSkeleton}`, so an override without that named export breaks PLP at import time.\n\n`zimmProductCardShared.ts` holds DOM-contract constants (`data-card-slot` names) + `CARD_BP` derived from\n`theme/foundation/gridSystem`. No design values, so it stays co-located (precedent:\n`zimmColourSwatchGroupShared.ts`). Keeps the Tier 2 surface at exactly one file.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Architecture decisions\n\n**Variation axis = discriminated union (data contract) + declarative config table (layout).**\n`typescript.md` mandates the union, and the ACs require it: Wishlist alone needs `colour` + `size` + mandatory\n`onRemove`; PLP alone needs `sizes` + `onNotifyMe`. A flat optional-everything bag lets you build a Wishlist\ncard with no remove CTA (AC violation) and a PLP card with one (design violation).\n\n```\nZimmProductCardProps = Base & (PlpVariation | HomeVariation | WishlistVariation)\n  base:       id, href, name, headingLevel, primaryImage, hoverImage?, images?,\n              currentPrice?, originalPrice?, isUnavailable?, badge?\n  'plp':      sizes?, selectedSize?, onSizeSelect?, onAddToBag?, onNotifyMe?\n  'home':     —\n  'wishlist': colour, size, onRemove (required), onAddToBag?\n```\n\n`'recently-viewed'` is **not** a fourth member — the AC says it behaves identically to PLP, so consumers pass\n`variation: 'plp'`. Recorded as an assumption, not a silent choice.\n\nThe render body must **not** `switch` on `props.variation`. Layout comes from a module-level `VARIATION_CONFIG`\n(`showBag`, `showAttributes`, `showRemove`, `hoverHidesPrice`, `hoverRevealsPrice`, `hoverRevealsSizeSelector`,\n`hoverSwapsImage`). That is what satisfies the extensibility AC: add a union member + a config row, touch no JSX.\n\n**The card is fluid, not 288px.** `gridSystem.ts` has `maxWidth: 'none'`. Figma's 288/179 are widths at the\nreference artboards. The card fills its grid cell (`width: 100%`); the image holds ratio via `aspectRatio`.\nGrid placement belongs to SFCC-80.\n\n**Mobile/desktop boundary = 768px** (`breakpoints.md`, 48em) — the only confirmed foundation breakpoint between\nthe 402 and 1440 artboards, and where the grid already goes 4→8 columns. `min-width` is inclusive, so the 768\nartboard gets the desktop card. Every hover rule is additionally gated on `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`\nso a 768px touch tablet never gets hover-only content. See OQ-5.\n\n**Hover is CSS-only, keyed on `:hover, :focus-within`.** No React hover state — 60 tiles re-rendering on pointer\nmove is a PLP perf defect. `:focus-within` is not optional: WCAG 2.2 AA requires the revealed size selector and\narrows be keyboard-reachable. All reveal selectors live in one place, the organism's recipe root part, selecting\ndescendants by the shared `data-card-slot` constants. Molecules stay hover-agnostic.\n\n**One link per card (stretched-link).** The card holds a bag button, arrows, size options and a remove button —\nnesting those in an `<a>` is invalid HTML and an a11y failure. The link wraps the product name only and carries\na `::after { position:absolute; inset:0 }` overlay; controls get `position:relative; z-index:1`. `href` arrives\npre-prefixed from the page. Uses `import {Link as RouteLink} from 'react-router-dom'` per `zimmColourSwatch` —\nthere is no `overrides/app/components/link/` override, contrary to a claim in `overrideStyling.md`.\n\n**Reuse, don't rebuild:** `ZimmProductName`, `ZimmProductPrice`, `ZimmIcon` (`bag`, `close`, `chevron-left`,\n`chevron-right` all exist in `ICON_MAP`). No new icons.\n\n**VIC pricing is not a card concern** — precedence is the page mapper's job. Deferred to SFCC-778 (OQ-6).\n\n---\n\n## 4a. Arrows vs. indicator — RESOLVED (Marcel, 2026-09-02)\n\n> *\"position indicator is just for tablet and mobile, desktop hover is arrows\"*\n\nThe two are **mutually exclusive**, not paired. Consequences for Steps 1 and 4:\n\n**Split by pointer capability, not viewport width.** The gate is\n`@media (hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` — the same gate every other hover rule already uses:\n\n- **hover-capable + fine pointer** → prev/next arrow buttons, revealed on `:hover, :focus-within`. No indicator.\n- **everything else (touch tablet, mobile)** → `ZimmSlideIndicator`, always visible. No arrows.\n\nCapability rather than width is what actually delivers the stated intent: the 768 artboard is a *touch* tablet,\nand a width rule alone would give it arrows it can never hover to reveal. It also keeps this consistent with the\ncard's own hover rules rather than introducing a second, differently-keyed breakpoint. **Edge case, accepted:**\na desktop browser resized below 768 keeps arrows (it still has a fine pointer); a touch-screen laptop gets the\nindicator. Both are the safe failure direction — nobody is shown a control they cannot operate.\n\n**Two knock-on changes to the step pass criteria:**\n\n1. **Step 1 — the indicator is NOT `aria-hidden` on touch.** The original criterion assumed the arrow buttons\n   announce position, so the indicator could be hidden as duplicate. Where the indicator renders there are **no\n   arrows**, so it becomes the only position signal — hiding it would be a 1.1.1 information loss. It needs an\n   accessible position announcement (e.g. a visually-hidden \"Image 3 of 8\" live region, or `role=\"group\"` with\n   `aria-label`). Revised criterion: exposed and announced when it is the sole affordance.\n2. **Step 4 — mobile image paging has no mechanism and no AC.** If the indicator shows position on touch, some\n   touch gesture must change the image. No AC covers this; the ACs only describe desktop hover. See OQ-11.\n\n---\n\n## 4b. Touch image paging — RESOLVED (Marcel, 2026-09-02)\n\n> *\"scroll-snap it is\"*\n\nNative CSS scroll-snap. The dots remain a **passive readout**, visually unchanged from Figma — no enlargement,\nno conversion to tap targets, so no departure from the design and no WCAG 2.5.8 problem to solve.\n\n**Implementation shape: one scroll container on every breakpoint, not two mechanisms.**\n\nThe image strip is always a horizontally scrollable flex container with `scrollSnapType: 'x mandatory'` and\n`scrollSnapAlign: 'start'` on each slide. What differs by pointer capability is only the *affordance* layered\nover it:\n\n| | Mechanism | Affordance |\n|---|---|---|\n| hover + fine pointer | same scroll container | arrows call `scrollBy()`; no indicator |\n| touch | same scroll container | native drag; indicator reflects position |\n\nOne container rather than two code paths means a single source of truth for \"which image is showing\", the\narrows become progressive enhancement over something that already works, and the desktop path inherits\nkeyboard scrollability for free.\n\n**Active-index derivation:** prefer `IntersectionObserver` on the slides over a scroll listener — 60 tiles on a\nPLP make a per-scroll-event listener a real perf cost, and the repo already has\n`overrides/app/hooks/use-intersection-observer.ts` (**Tier 2 — import it, do not modify it**). The `scrollend`\nevent is an acceptable alternative. Left to the Implementer; either satisfies the pass criteria.\n\n**Precedent:** the base `ProductScroller`\n(`node_modules/@salesforce/retail-react-app/app/components/product-scroller/index.jsx:79-84`) already uses\n`scrollSnapType: 'x mandatory'` with `overflowX: 'scroll'`. Same technique, already proven in this codebase.\n\n**Accepted caveat:** swipe has no explicit affordance beyond the dots implying further content. This is the\nuniversal mobile convention, and the alternative (enlarging 1px dots into 24×24 tap targets) trades an\ninvisible-but-expected gesture for a visible-but-off-design control. **Action: flag to Kiera that the mobile\ngesture is undocumented in the design**, so it lands in the spec rather than being discovered in QA.\n\n**Added Step 4 pass criteria:**\n- The slide container carries `scrollSnapType: 'x mandatory'`; every slide carries `scrollSnapAlign`.\n- Arrows are rendered only under `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`; the indicator only outside it; assert the\n  two never coexist in the same DOM.\n- Arrow activation moves the scroll position (assert `scrollBy`/`scrollTo` is called — jsdom does not lay out,\n  so assert the call, not the resulting offset).\n- The indicator's active index tracks the observed slide, and clamps at both ends.\n- A single-image product renders no indicator, no arrows, and no horizontal overflow.\n- No gesture library is added — `package.json` is untouched (AGENTS.md Hard Rule #7).\n\n---\n\n## 4c. Badge — RESOLVED (Marcel, 2026-09-02), and it corrects the decomposition\n\nNine nodes supplied; all resolve off one parent, `40001471:20702` \"Badge\" (a documentation board).\n`40001471:20682` is its cover slide, not a component.\n\n**There are TWO badge components, not one:**\n\n| Component set | Node | Variants | Size |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **`Badge`** | `40001471:20752` | `State=Primary` (`…20751`) · `State=Hover` (`…20750`) | 33×15 |\n| **`Stock Status Badge`** | `40001796:7041` | `State=Available` (`…7040`) · `State=Unavailable` (`…7039`) · `State=Low stock` (`…7038`) | 66–79×20 |\n\n`Stock Status Badge` appears in **no acceptance criterion**. It maps to `.docs/prd/plp.md` §3\n(\"**OUT OF STOCK** shown on tile when all sizes are at or below buffer threshold\" and the low-stock\nindicator) — so the PRD requirement that had no design **does** have one. Its `Low stock` state\ncorresponds to nothing in any AC, and `plp.md` §3 places low-stock *at size level*, not on the tile.\n\n### Decomposition correction — three slots move from Content to Media\n\nReading `Image Group / Size=Large` (`40002742:46322`, 288×432) and `Size=Small` (`40002742:46321`,\n179×268) shows the overlays live on the **image**, not in the text block:\n\n| Slot | Frame | Structure | Desktop | Mobile |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| **`Tag`** (the badge) | 78×48 @ top-left | `touchContainer` 30×24 @ (24,12) → `Label` text 30×8 | ✅ | ✅ |\n| **`Close`** (remove 'x') | 48×48 @ top-right | `touchContainer` 24×24 → `Icon` 16×16 | ✅ | ✅ |\n| **`Carousel`** (arrows) | 288×24 @ y=204 (vertically centred) | two `touchContainer` 24×24 @ x=12 and x=252 | ✅ | **absent** |\n| **`Slide-indicator`** | 179×1, flush to the image's bottom edge (y=267 of 268) | instance | **absent** | ✅ |\n\n**Consequences:**\n\n1. **The badge slot moves from Step 5 to Step 4.** It is `Tag`, overlaid top-left on the image — not a part\n   of `Card-content`. Remove the `badge` part from `zimmProductCardContent.css.ts`; add a `tag` part to\n   `zimmProductCardMedia.css.ts`.\n2. **The remove ('x') CTA moves from Step 5 to Step 4.** It is `Close`, top-right on the image. The plan had\n   it in `ZimmProductCardContent` — wrong molecule. Remove `onRemove` handling from Content; it belongs in\n   Media. The union still carries `onRemove` on the wishlist member; the organism passes it to Media.\n3. **This makes the split cleaner, not messier:** everything overlaid on the image is Media, everything below\n   it is Content.\n4. **§4a and §4b are confirmed by the design itself,** not just by the verbal decision — `Size=Large` has\n   `Carousel` and no indicator; `Size=Small` has `Slide-indicator` and no `Carousel`.\n5. **The slide indicator is a 1px hairline flush to the image's bottom edge**, not floating dots. Step 1's\n   recipe must not assume dot geometry.\n\n### Two new findings that need raising, not resolving in code\n\n- **🟡 The badge looks interactive, against a requirement that says it must not be.** `Tag` wraps its label in\n  a **`touchContainer`** — this file's convention for an interactive hit area (the bag icon and both arrows use\n  the identical wrapper) — and the `Badge` component ships a **`State=Hover`**. But SPR-16 reads\n  \"~~clickable~~ product badges\" and `.docs/prd/plp.md:70` says clickable badges are *\"a NEW unsigned request —\n  not confirmed… **Do not implement**.\"* Two independent signals in Figma versus an explicit written\n  prohibition. **Build it non-interactive per the requirement** (a plain element, no button, no hover rule),\n  and raise the discrepancy — do not resolve it in code.\n- **🔵 `Icons OLD`.** The `Carousel` arrows in the current card design instance icons named\n  `Icons OLD` (`40003066:50796`, `40003066:50798`). Deprecated naming inside a live component. Flag to Kiera.\n- **🔵 The card's `Tag` does not instance the `Badge` component.** `Tag`'s child is a raw `Label` text node\n  (30×8), not an instance of `40001471:20752` (33×15). The design system has a Badge component the card\n  doesn't use. Ask which is authoritative before styling the slot.\n\n### Revised Step 4 pass criteria (additions)\n\n- ~~`Tag` renders top-left when `badge` is supplied~~ **REMOVED (§4e)** — no confirmed design requires it; cut entirely, not deferred.\n- `Close` renders top-right **only** for the wishlist variation, is a native `<button>` with an `aria-label`\n  naming the product, hit area ≥ 24×24, and calls `onRemove`.\n- Stock status is **out of scope for SFCC-236** unless the developer says otherwise — it is unmentioned in\n  every AC, and its `Low stock` state contradicts `plp.md` §3's size-level placement. See OQ-12.\n\n---\n\n## 4d. Against the ticket\n\n**🔴 must-fix, CONFIRMED, UNIFORM (same defect shape twice in one file)** — `zimmProductCard/index.tsx`:\n\n1. **Lines ~27-33**, the module docblock states, in its own words: Wishlist's hover reveal (a full size-chip-selector swap) *\"is NOT what the SFCC-236 Jira AC text says (it only mentions the image switch + arrows for Wishlist hover), but the design is the source of truth here per AGENTS.md's doc-integrity rule.\"* I read AGENTS.md's actual doc-integrity rule — it says the opposite of what it's cited for: *\"If a user instruction contradicts [a PRD/design]... pause immediately... and ask the user whether the doc should be updated or the instruction is a deliberate override.
